Homotopy similarity of maps. Strong similarity
S. S. Podkorytov

Abstract
Given pointed cellular spaces X X and Y Y , X X compact, and an integer r ⩾ 0 r\geqslant 0 , we define a relation ≈ 𝑟 \overset{r}{\approx} on [ X , Y ] [X,Y] and argue for the conjecture that it always coincides with the r r -similarity ∼ 𝑟 \overset{r}{\sim} .



§ 1. Introduction

This paper continues
[
2
]
.
We adopt notation and conventions thereof.
Let
X
and
Y
be cellular spaces,

X
compact.
For each
r\geqslant 0
,
we define a relation
\overset{r}{\approx}
,
called the strong
r
-similarity,
on the set
[X,Y]
.
We will need it in our next paper
[
4
]
.
We conjecture that
strong
r
-similarity always coincides with

r
-similarity
\overset{r}{\sim}
.
It follows immediately from the definition that
it implies
r
-similarity and
gets nonstrictly stronger as
r
grows.
We do not know whether
the strong
r
-similarity is always an equivalence.
We prove this in the case
X=\Sigma T

(§ 8).
The main results are as follows.
Strong
1
-similarity coincides with
1
-similarity
(Theorem 14.2).
(We believe that

1
-similarity can be given a homological characterization
similar to that of homotopy invariants of order at most
1

[
1
]
.)
If
X=S^{1}
,
the strong
r
-similarity coincides with the
r
-similarity
(§ 24).
All
(r+1)
-fold Whitehead products are strongly

r
-similar to zero
(Theorem 27.2).

§ 2. Definition of strong similarity

Augmentation.

For a set
V
,
introduce the homomorphism

\epsilon:\langle V\rangle\to{\mathbb{Z}},\qquad{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}v{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}\mapsto 1,

the
augmentation
.
An ensemble
S\in\langle V\rangle
is called
affine
if

\epsilon(S)=1
.

The hash product.

Given
a wedge

T=\bigvee_{i\in(m)}T_{i}

(hereafter,

(m)=\{1,\dotsc,m\}
)
and
a space
Z
,
we have the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-multilinear operation

\mathop{\sbighash}_{i\in(m)}:\prod_{i\in(m)}\langle Z^{T_{i}}\rangle\to\langle Z^{T}\rangle,\qquad\mathop{\sbighash}_{i\in(m)}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}v_{i}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.86108pt\vskip 0.99025pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.99025pt\hrule height=0.86108pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.6458pt\vskip 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 1.07639pt\hrule height=0.6458pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.55974pt\vskip 0.90419pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.90419pt\hrule height=0.55974pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.38748pt\vskip 0.6458pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.6458pt\hrule height=0.38748pt}}}}_{i\in(m)}v_{i}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}.

Simplex and its faces.

Fix a nonempty finite set
E
.
Let
{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)
be the set of nonempty subsets
F\subseteq E
.
Let
{\mathscr{A}}(E)
be the set of subsets

A\subseteq{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)
such that
all
F\in A
are disjoint
(
layouts
).

Let
\Delta E
be the simplex spanned by
E
.
For
F\in{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)
,

\Delta F\subseteq\Delta E
is a face.
For
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
put

\Delta[A]=\bigcup_{F\in A}\Delta F\subseteq\Delta E.

Fissile ensembles.

For
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
we have

\Delta[A]_{+}=\bigvee_{F\in A}(\Delta F)_{+}.

Hereafter,

U_{+}=U\sqcup\{{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\}
.
Given a space
Z
,
we call an ensemble
S\in\langle Z^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle

fissile

if,
for each
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,

S|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}S|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}

(1)

in
\langle Z^{\Delta[A]_{+}}\rangle
.

An ensemble of the form
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}v{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}
is fissile.
A fissile ensemble is affine
(take
A=\varnothing
in the definition).
An affine ensemble
S
is fissile if
it satisfies (
1
)
for all
A

with
|A|=2
.
Given a space
\widetilde{Z}\supseteq Z
,
we have
\langle\widetilde{Z}^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\supseteq\langle Z^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle
;
the ensemble
S
is fissile
as an element of
\langle\widetilde{Z}^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle

if and only if
it is fissile
as an element of
\langle Z^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle
.

Spaces of maps.

Let

X

and

Y

be spaces,

X
compact Hausdorff.
Then

Y^{X}
is the space of maps
X\to Y
;
its basepoint is the constant map
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}^{X}_{Y}
.
Given a map
a:X\to Y
,
we define the spaces

(Y^{X},a)
as
Y^{X}
with the basepoint moved to
a

and

Y^{X}_{a}
as the basepoint path component of
(Y^{X},a)
.
For an unpointed space
U
,
introduce the function

\theta^{U}_{a}:Y^{X}\to(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}},\qquad\theta^{U}_{a}(d)(u)=d\quad(u\in U),\quad\theta^{U}_{a}(d)({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}})=a.

The filtration
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}
.

Let
T
be a space.
The function

\square^{X}:(Y^{X},a)^{T}\to Y^{T\times X},\qquad\square^{X}(v)(t,x)=v(t)(x),

induces the homomorphism

\langle\square^{X}\rangle:\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle\to\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle.

The filtration of
\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle

(see
[
2
]
)
induces a filtration of
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle
:

\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}=\langle\square^{X}\rangle^{-1}(\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle^{(s)}).

Strong similarity.

Let

X

and

Y

be cellular spaces,

X
compact,
and

a,b:X\to Y
be maps.
We adopt the inclusion
\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{T}\rangle\subseteq\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle
.
We say that
a
is
strongly
r
-similar
to
b
,

a\overset{r}{\approx}b,

if,
for any nonempty finite set
E
,
there exists a fissile ensemble

S\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle
such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

We have
a\overset{r}{\approx}a

(put
S={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(a){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}
).
Clearly,

a\overset{r}{\approx}b
implies
a\overset{r}{\sim}b

(take
E=\{\bullet\}
).
We prove below
(Theorem 6.2)
that the relation
\overset{r}{\approx}
is homotopy invariant.

§ 3. On the filtration
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}

Let

X
,

Y
,

T

and
\widetilde{T}

be spaces,

X

compact Hausdorff,
and

a:X\to Y

and

k:\widetilde{T}\to T

be maps.
Then the homomorphism

\langle(Y^{X},a)^{k}\rangle:\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle\to\langle(Y^{X},a)^{\widetilde{T}}\rangle

takes

\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}

to

\langle(Y^{X},a)^{\widetilde{T}}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}
.

We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\langle(Y^{X},a)^{k}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle Y^{k\times{\mathrm{id}}_{X}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X},a)^{\widetilde{T}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{\widetilde{T}\times X}\rangle.}

By the definition of
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}
,

\langle\square^{X}\rangle
takes
it
to

\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle^{(s)}
.
By
[
3
, Lemma 2.1]
,

\langle Y^{k\times{\mathrm{id}}_{X}}\rangle

takes
the latter
to

\langle Y^{\widetilde{T}\times X}\rangle^{(s)}
.
By commutativity of the diagram,

\langle(Y^{X},a)^{k}\rangle

takes

\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}

to

\langle\square^{X}\rangle^{-1}(\langle Y^{\widetilde{T}\times X}\rangle^{(s)})
,
which is
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{\widetilde{T}}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}

by the definition of the latter.
∎

The case
a={\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}^{X}_{Y}
.

Let

X
,

Y
,
and
\widetilde{X}

be spaces,

X

and

\widetilde{X}

compact Hausdorff,
and

k:\widetilde{X}\to X
be a surjective map.
Then the homomorphism

\langle Y^{k}\rangle:\langle Y^{X}\rangle\to\langle Y^{\widetilde{X}}\rangle

satisfies

\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(s)}=\langle Y^{k}\rangle^{-1}(\langle Y^{\widetilde{X}}\rangle^{(s)}).

(2)

By
[
3
, Lemma 2.1]
,

\langle Y^{k}\rangle
preserves the filtration,
which yields the inclusion
\subseteq
in (
2
).
Check the inclusion
\supseteq
.
Take
V\in\langle Y^{k}\rangle^{-1}(\langle Y^{\widetilde{X}}\rangle^{(s)})

and
show that

V\in\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(s)}
.
Take
R\in{\mathscr{F}}_{s-1}(X)
.
We should check that

V|_{R}=0
.
We have
R=k(Q)

for some
Q\in{\mathscr{F}}_{s-1}(\widetilde{X})
.
Since
\langle Y^{k}\rangle(V)\in\langle Y^{\widetilde{X}}\rangle^{(s)}
,
we have
\langle Y^{k}\rangle(V)|_{Q}=0
.
We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle V}}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{R}}
\scriptstyle{\langle Y^{k}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{R}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle Y^{h}\rangle}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle V|_{R}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\langle Y^{k}\rangle(V)}}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{\widetilde{X}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{Q}}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{Q}\rangle,}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle 0}}

where
h=k|_{Q\to R}
.
Since
h
is surjective,

\langle Y^{h}\rangle
is injective.
Thus

V|_{R}=0
.
∎

Let

X
,

Y

and

T

be spaces,

X

compact Hausdorff.
Let

{\widehat{\square}}^{X}:(Y^{X})^{T}\to Y^{T\wedge X}

be the standard bijection.
Consider the homomorphism

\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X}\rangle:\langle(Y^{X})^{T}\rangle\to\langle Y^{T\wedge X}\rangle.

One has

\langle(Y^{X})^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}=\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X}\rangle^{-1}(\langle Y^{T\wedge X}\rangle^{(s)}).

We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X})^{T}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{T\wedge X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle Y^{k}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle,}

where

k:T\times X\to T\wedge X
is the projection.
By definition,

\langle(Y^{X})^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}=\langle\square^{X}\rangle^{-1}(\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle^{(s)}).

By Lemma 3.2,

\langle Y^{T\wedge X}\rangle^{(s)}=\langle Y^{k}\rangle^{-1}(\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle^{(s)}).

The desired equality follows.
∎

§ 4. Primitive transforms

Let

Z

and

\widetilde{Z}

be spaces
and

g:Z\to\widetilde{Z}
be a map.
For a space
T
,
we have the function
g^{T}:Z^{T}\to{\widetilde{Z}}^{T}
.

Let
E
be a nonempty finite set.
Consider the homomorphism

\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle:\langle Z^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\to\langle{\widetilde{Z}}^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle.

Then,
for any fissile ensemble
S\in\langle Z^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle
,
the ensemble
\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S)
is fissile.

Take
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
.
We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(S)_{F\in A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1)}
\scriptstyle{(2)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S))_{F\in A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(3)}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\langle Z^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}{?}|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\langle{\widetilde{Z}}^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}{?}|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(S|_{(\Delta F)_{+}})_{F\in A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(4)}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\langle Z^{(\Delta F)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\langle g^{(\Delta F)_{+}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\sbighash}\limits_{F\in A}}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\langle{\widetilde{Z}}^{(\Delta F)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\sbighash}\limits_{F\in A}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S)|_{(\Delta F)_{+}})_{F\in A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(5)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle S|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}}}
\textstyle{\langle Z^{\Delta[A]_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle g^{\Delta[A]_{+}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle{\widetilde{Z}}^{\Delta[A]_{+}}\rangle}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\mathop{\sbighash}\limits_{F\in A}\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S)|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}}}
\textstyle{\langle Z^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}}
\scriptstyle{\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle{\widetilde{Z}}^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(6)}
\scriptstyle{(8)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(7)}

The sending (4) is fissility of
S
.
The sendings (1), (2), (3), (5), (6), and (8) are obvious.
The sending (7) follows.
It is fissility of
\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S)
.
∎

Primitivity.

Let

X
,

Y
,

\widetilde{X}
,
and

\widetilde{Y}

be spaces,

X

and

\widetilde{X}

compact Hausdorff,
and

g:Y^{X}\to{\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}}

be an unbased map
(a
transform
).
We suppose that
the transform
g
is

primitive
:
for each point
w\in\widetilde{X}
,
there is
a point
k(w)\in X

and
an unbased map
h^{w}:Y\to\widetilde{Y}

such that

g(d)(w)=h^{w}(d(k(w))),\qquad d\in Y^{X}.

For a map
a:X\to Y
,
we have the map
g:(Y^{X},a)\to({\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}},g(a))
.

For
a map
a:X\to Y

and
a space
T
,
the homomorphism
\langle g^{T}\rangle

takes
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}

to
\langle({\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}},g(a))^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{\widetilde{X}}
.

We may assume that

k({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}_{\widetilde{X}})={\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}_{X}

and

h^{{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}_{\widetilde{X}}}({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}_{Y})={\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}_{\widetilde{Y}}
.
We have the function

K={\mathrm{id}}\times k:T\times\widetilde{X}\to T\times X.

For
Q\in{\mathscr{F}}_{s-1}(T\times\widetilde{X})
,
we have
K(Q)\in{\mathscr{F}}_{s-1}(T\times X)
.
We have
the function

H:Y^{K(Q)}\to{\widetilde{Y}}^{Q},\qquad H(u)(t,w)=h^{w}(u(K(t,w))),\ (t,w)\in Q,\ u\in Y^{K(Q)},

and
the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\langle g^{T}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{T\times X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{K(Q)}}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{K(Q)}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle H\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle({\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}},g(a))^{T}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{\widetilde{X}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle{\widetilde{Y}}^{T\times\widetilde{X}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{Q}}
\textstyle{\langle{\widetilde{Y}}^{Q}\rangle.}

By the definition of
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}
,
it goes to zero
under the composition in the upper row.
Thus
its image under
\langle g^{T}\rangle

goes to zero
under the composition in the lower row.
Since
Q
was taken arbitrarily,
this image is contained in
\langle({\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}},g(a))^{T}\rangle^{(s)}_{\widetilde{X}}
.
∎

Suppose that

X
,

Y
,

\widetilde{X}
,
and

\widetilde{Y}

are cellular.

Let
a,b:X\to Y
be maps such that

a\overset{r}{\approx}b
.
Then

g(a)\overset{r}{\approx}g(b)
.

Take a finite set
E
.
We have a fissile ensemble
S\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle
such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

Consider the homomorphism

\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle:\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\to\langle({\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}},g(a))^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle.

Since

\theta^{\Delta E}_{g(a)}(g(b))=g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}(\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b)),

we have

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{g(a)}(g(b)){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S)=\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S),

which belongs to

\langle({\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}},g(a))^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{\widetilde{X}}

by Lemma 4.2.
By Lemma 4.1,
the ensemble
\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S)
is fissile.
Since
g
is continuous,
it takes
Y^{X}_{a}
to
{\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}}_{g(a)}
.
Thus

\langle g^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle(S)\in\langle({\widetilde{Y}}^{\widetilde{X}}_{g(a)})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle.

We are done.
∎

§ 5. Compositions and smash products

Compositions.

Let

X
,

Y
,

\widetilde{X}
,
and

\widetilde{Y}

be cellular spaces,

X

and

\widetilde{X}

compact.

Let

k:\widetilde{X}\to X

and

h:Y\to\widetilde{Y}

be maps
and

a,b:X\to Y
be maps such that
a\overset{r}{\approx}b
.
Then

a\circ k\overset{r}{\approx}b\circ k
in
Y^{\widetilde{X}}

and

h\circ a\overset{r}{\approx}h\circ b
in
{\widetilde{Y}}^{X}
.

The transforms

Y^{X}\to Y^{\widetilde{X}},\qquad d\mapsto d\circ k,

and

Y^{X}\to{\widetilde{Y}}^{X},\qquad d\mapsto h\circ d,

are primitive.
By Lemma 4.3,
they preserve strong
r
-similarity.
∎

Let

k:\widetilde{X}\to X

and

h:Y\to\widetilde{Y}

be maps
and

a:X\to Y
be a map such that
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a
.
Then

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a\circ k
in
Y^{\widetilde{X}}

and

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}h\circ a
in
{\widetilde{Y}}^{X}
.

Follows from Corollary 5.1.

Smash products.

Let

X
,

Y
,
and

T

be cellular spaces,

X

and

T

compact.

Let

a,b:X\to Y
be maps such that
a\overset{r}{\approx}b
.
Then
the maps

a\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T},b\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T}:X\wedge T\to Y\wedge T

satisfy
a\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T}\overset{r}{\approx}b\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T}
.

The transform

Y^{X}\to(Y\wedge T)^{X\wedge T},\qquad d\mapsto d\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T},

is primitive.
By Lemma 4.3,
it preserves strong
r
-similarity.
∎

Let

a:X\to Y
be a map such that
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a
.
Then
the map

a\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T}:X\wedge T\to Y\wedge T

satisfies
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T}
.

Follows from Corollary 5.3.

§ 6. Homotopy invariance

Let

X

and

Y

be cellular spaces,

X

compact.

Let maps
a,b,\widetilde{a}:X\to Y
satisfy

\widetilde{a}\sim a\overset{r}{\approx}b.

Then
\widetilde{a}\overset{r}{\approx}b
.

By definition,
the relation
\overset{r}{\approx}
tolerates homotopy of its left argument.
In detail.
For an unbased space
U
,
we have the bijection

e^{U}:(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\to(Y^{X},\widetilde{a})^{U_{+}},\qquad e^{U}(v)\mathrel{{=}|_{U}}v,\quad e^{U}(v)({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}})=\widetilde{a}.

Clearly,

e^{U}(\theta^{U}_{a}(d))=\theta^{U}_{\widetilde{a}}(d),\qquad d\in Y^{X}.

(3)

Since
a\sim\widetilde{a}
,

e^{U}
takes

(Y^{X}_{a})^{U_{+}}

to

(Y^{X}_{\widetilde{a}})^{U_{+}}
.

The homomorphism

\langle e^{U}\rangle:\langle(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\rangle\to\langle(Y^{X},\widetilde{a})^{U_{+}}\rangle

takes

\langle(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}

to

\langle(Y^{X},\widetilde{a})^{U_{+}}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}
.
Indeed,
we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle e^{U}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X},\widetilde{a})^{U_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{U_{+}\times X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.1507pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\widetilde{a}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.1507pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{(U_{+}\times X)\vee X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle Y^{k}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{U_{+}\times X}\rangle,}

where
k:U_{+}\times X\to(U_{+}\times X)\vee X
is given by the rules

k(u,x)={\mathrm{in}}_{1}(u,x),\qquad k({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}},x)={\mathrm{in}}_{2}(x),\qquad u\in U,\ x\in X.

In the lower row,

\langle Y^{U_{+}\times X}\rangle^{(s)}

goes
to
\langle Y^{(U_{+}\times X)\vee X}\rangle^{(s)}\rangle

by
[
3
, Lemma 3.1]

and
then
to

\langle Y^{U_{+}\times X}\rangle^{(s)}

by
[
3
, Lemma 2.1]
.
This suffices
by the definition of

\langle(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}

and

\langle(Y^{X},\widetilde{a})^{U_{+}}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}
.

Take a nonempty finite set
E
.
For

A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)

and
a collection
S_{F}\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta F)_{+}}\rangle
,

F\in A
,
we have

\langle e^{\Delta[A]}\rangle(\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}S_{F})=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}e^{\Delta F}(S_{F})

(4)

in
\langle(Y^{X},\widetilde{a})^{\Delta[F]_{+}}\rangle
.
We have a fissile ensemble
S\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle

such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}.

(5)

We get the ensemble

\langle e^{\Delta E}\rangle(S)\in\langle(Y^{X}_{\widetilde{a}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle
,
which is fissile.
Indeed,
for
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
we have

\langle e^{\Delta E}\rangle(S)|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}\overset{(*)}{=}\langle e^{\Delta[A]}\rangle(S|_{\Delta[A]_{+}})=\\
\qquad\text{(since $S$ is fissile)}\hfill=\langle e^{\Delta[A]}\rangle\bigl(\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}S|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}\bigr)=\hfill\text{(by \eqref{e-hash})}\qquad\\
=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}\langle e^{\Delta F}\rangle(S|_{(\Delta F)_{+}})\overset{(*)}{=}\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}\langle e^{\Delta E}\rangle(S)|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}

(the equalities
(*)
hold by naturality of
e^{U}
with respect
to
U
).
We have

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\widetilde{a}}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-\langle e^{\Delta E}\rangle(S)=\hfill\text{(by \eqref{e-theta})}\qquad\\
=\langle e^{\Delta E}\rangle({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S)\in\langle(Y^{X}_{\widetilde{a}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)},

where
\in
follows from (
5
)
because
\langle e^{\Delta E}\rangle
preserves the filtration.
Thus

\widetilde{a}\overset{r}{\approx}b
.
∎

Let maps
a,b,\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}:X\to Y
satisfy

\widetilde{a}\sim a\overset{r}{\approx}b\sim\widetilde{b}.

Then
\widetilde{a}\overset{r}{\approx}\widetilde{b}
.

We
crop
Y

and
assume it compact.
By
[
2
, Corollary 4.2]
,
we can continuously associate
to each path
v:[0,1]\to Y

an unbased homotopy
E_{t}(v):Y\to Y
,
t\in[0,1]
, such that

E_{0}(v)={\mathrm{id}}

and

E_{t}(v)(v(0))=v(t)
.
Let
h_{t}:X\to Y
,
t\in[0,1]
, be a homotopy such that

h_{0}=b

and

h_{1}=\widetilde{b}
.
For
x\in X
,
introduce the path
v_{x}=h_{?}(x):[0,1]\to Y
.
We have

v_{x}(0)=h_{0}(x)=b(x)

and

v_{x}(1)=h_{1}(x)=\widetilde{b}(x)
.
Introduce the homotopy

H_{t}:X\times Y\to Y,\ t\in[0,1],\qquad H_{t}(x,y)=E_{t}(v_{x})(y).

We have

H_{0}(x,y)=E_{0}(v_{x})(y)=y

and

H_{1}(x,b(x))=E_{1}(v_{x})(b(x))=E_{1}(v_{x})(v_{x}(0))=v_{x}(1)=\widetilde{b}(x).

Consider the primitive transforms

g_{t}:Y^{X}\to Y^{X},\ t\in[0,1],\qquad g_{t}(d)(x)=H_{t}(x,d(x)).

We have

d=g_{0}(d)\sim g_{1}(d)
,

d\in Y^{X}
,
and

g_{1}(b)=\widetilde{b}
.

We have

\widetilde{a}\sim a\sim g_{1}(a)\overset{r}{\approx}g_{1}(b)=\widetilde{b},

where
\overset{r}{\approx}
holds by Lemma 4.3.
By Lemma 6.1,

\widetilde{a}\overset{r}{\approx}\widetilde{b}
.
∎

Using Theorem 6.2,
we define the relation of strong
r
-similarity
on the set
[X,Y]

by the rule

[a]\overset{r}{\approx}[b]\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad a\overset{r}{\approx}b.

§ 7. Joining ensembles

Let

X_{1}
,

X_{2}
,

Y
,
and

T

be spaces,

X_{i}

compact Hausdorff.
Consider the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-bilinear operation

\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{T}:\langle(Y^{X_{1}})^{T}\rangle\times\langle(Y^{X_{2}})^{T}\rangle\to\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{T}\rangle,\qquad{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}v_{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{T}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}v_{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}v{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}},\\
v(t)=v_{1}(t)\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}v_{2}(t):X_{1}\vee X_{2}\to Y,\qquad t\in T.

Let

E
be a finite set
and

S_{i}\in\langle(Y^{X_{i}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle
,

i=1,2
,
be fissile ensembles.
Then
the ensemble

S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2}\in\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle

is fissile.

Take
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
.
We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}(\langle(Y^{X_{1}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\times\langle(Y^{X_{2}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}({?}|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}\times{?}|_{(\Delta F)_{+}})}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}{?}|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}(\langle(Y^{X_{1}})^{(\Delta F)_{+}}\rangle\times\langle(Y^{X_{2}})^{(\Delta F)_{+}}\rangle)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta F)_{+}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\sbighash}\limits_{F\in A}\times\mathop{\sbighash}\limits_{F\in A}}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{F\in A}\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{(\Delta F)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\sbighash}\limits_{F\in A}}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X_{1}})^{\Delta[A]_{+}}\rangle\times\langle(Y^{X_{2}})^{\Delta[F]_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{\Delta[A]_{+}}}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{\Delta[A]_{+}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X_{1}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\times\langle(Y^{X_{2}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}\times{?}|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}}

with the sendings

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle((S_{1},S_{2}))_{F\in A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1)}
\scriptstyle{(2)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2})_{F\in A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(3)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle((S_{1}|_{(\Delta F)_{+}},S_{2}|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}))_{F\in A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(4)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle((S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2})|_{(\Delta F)_{+}})_{F\in A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(5)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(S_{1}|_{\Delta[A]_{+}},S_{2}|_{\Delta[A]_{+}})}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\mathop{\sbighash}\limits_{F\in A}(S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2})|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(S_{1},S_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(6)}
\scriptstyle{(8)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2}.}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(7)}

The sending (4) holds by fissility of

S_{1}

and

S_{2}
.
The sendings (1), (2), (3), (5), (6), and (8) are obvious.
The sending (7) follows.
Thus
S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2}
is fissile.
∎

We have

\langle(Y^{X_{1}})^{T}\rangle^{(p)}_{X_{1}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{T}\langle(Y^{X_{2}})^{T}\rangle^{(q)}_{X_{2}}\subseteq\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{T}\rangle^{(p+q)}_{X_{1}\vee X_{2}}.

Take ensembles

Z_{1}\in\langle(Y^{X_{1}})^{T}\rangle^{(p)}_{X_{1}},\qquad Z_{2}\in\langle(Y^{X_{2}})^{T}\rangle^{(q)}_{X_{2}}.

We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X_{1}})^{T}\rangle\times\langle(Y^{X_{2}})^{T}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{1}}\rangle\times\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{2}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{T}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{T\wedge X_{1}}\rangle\times\langle Y^{T\wedge X_{2}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{(T\wedge X_{1})\vee(T\wedge X_{2})}\rangle}

(we used distributivity of the smash product over the wedge)
and
the sendings

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(Z_{1},Z_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle Z_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{T}Z_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{1}}\rangle(Z_{1}),\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{2}}\rangle(Z_{2}))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}}\rangle(Z_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{T}Z_{2}).}}

By Lemma 3.3,

(\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{1}}\rangle(Z_{1}),\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{2}}\rangle(Z_{2}))\in\langle Y^{T\wedge X_{1}}\rangle^{(p)}\times\langle Y^{T\wedge X_{2}}\rangle^{(q)}.

Thus,
by
[
3
, Lemma 3.1]
,

\langle{\widehat{\square}}^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}}\rangle(Z_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{T}Z_{2})\in\langle Y^{(T\wedge X_{1})\vee(T\wedge X_{2})}\rangle^{(p+q)}.

Thus,
by Lemma 3.3,

Z_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{T}Z_{2}\in\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{T}\rangle^{(p+q)}.\displaymath@qed

Let

X_{1}
,

X_{2}
,
and

Y

be cellular spaces,

X_{i}

compact.

Let maps
a_{i}:X_{i}\to Y
,

i=1,2
,
satisfy
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a_{i}
.
Then
the map

a_{1}\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}a_{2}:X_{1}\vee X_{2}\to Y

satisfies
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a_{1}\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}a_{2}
.

Take a finite set
E
.
We have fissile ensembles
S_{i}\in\langle(Y^{X_{i}}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle
,

i=1,2
,
such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(a_{i}){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S_{i}\in\langle(Y^{X_{i}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X_{i}}.

By Lemma 7.1,
the ensemble

S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2}\in\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle

is fissile.
We have

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(a_{1}\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}a_{2}){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2}=\\
={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(a_{1}){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(a_{2}){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}S_{2}=\\
=({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(a_{1}){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S_{1})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(a_{2}){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}+{}\\
+S_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}_{(\Delta E)_{+}}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(a_{2}){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S_{2})\in\langle(Y^{X_{1}\vee X_{2}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X_{1}\vee X_{2}},

where
\in
holds by Lemma 7.2.
We are done.
∎

§ 8. Strong similarity for an admissible couple

Let
X
and
Y
be cellular spaces,

X
compact.
Let
X
be equipped with maps

\mu:X\to X\vee X
(comultiplication) and

\nu:X\to X
(coinversion).
The set
Y^{X}
carries the operations

(a,b)\mapsto(a\mathbin{*}b:X\xrightarrow{\mu}X\vee X\xrightarrow{a\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}b}Y)

and

a\mapsto(a^{\dagger}:X\xrightarrow{\nu}X\xrightarrow{a}Y).

We suppose that

(X,\mu,\nu;Y)
is an admissible couple
in the sense of
[
3
]
,
that is,
the set
[X,Y]
is a group with
the multiplication

[a][b]=[a\mathbin{*}b],

the inversion

[a]^{-1}=[a^{\dagger}],

and
the identity
1=[{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}^{X}_{Y}]
.
We are mainly interested in the case of

X=\Sigma T
with standard

\mu

and

\nu
.

We proceed parallelly to
[
3
]
.
The subsets

[X,Y]^{((r+1))}=\{\,\boldsymbol{a}\in[X,Y]\mid 1\overset{r}{\approx}\boldsymbol{a}\,\}

form the filtration

[X,Y]=[X,Y]^{((1))}\supseteq[X,Y]^{((2))}\supseteq\dotso.

[X,Y]^{((r+1))}\subseteq[X,Y]
is a normal subgroup.

Take
a,b:X\to Y
,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a,b
.
Check that

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a*b
.
We have the decomposition

a*b:X\xrightarrow{\mu}X\vee X\xrightarrow{a\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}b}Y.

By Corollary 7.3,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}^{X\vee X}_{Y}\overset{r}{\approx}a\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}b
.
By Corollary 5.2,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a*b
.

Take
a:X\to Y
,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a
.
Check that

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a^{\dagger}
.
We have the decomposition

a^{\dagger}:X\xrightarrow{\nu}X\xrightarrow{a}Y.

By Corollary 5.2,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a^{\dagger}
.

Take
a,b:X\to Y
,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}a
.
Check that

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}b^{\dagger}*(a*b)
.
Consider the primitive transform

Y^{X}\to Y^{X},\qquad d\mapsto b^{\dagger}*(d*b).

We have

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}^{X}_{Y}\sim b^{\dagger}*({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}^{X}_{Y}*b)\overset{r}{\approx}b^{\dagger}*(a*b),

where
\overset{r}{\approx}
holds by Lemma 4.3.
By
Lemma 6.1,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}b^{\dagger}*(a*b)
.
∎

We do not know whether
the subgroups
[X,Y]^{((s))}
form an N-series.

For
\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in[X,Y]
,
we have

\boldsymbol{a}\overset{r}{\approx}\boldsymbol{b}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\boldsymbol{a}^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\in[X,Y]^{((r+1))}.

It suffices to check that,
for maps
a,b,c:X\to Y
,

a\overset{r}{\approx}b

implies

c*a\overset{r}{\approx}c*b
.
This follows from Lemma 4.3 for the primitive transform

Y^{X}\to Y^{X},\qquad d\mapsto c*d.\displaymath@qed

It follows from Theorems 8.1 and 8.2 that,
for an admissible couple
(X,\mu,\nu;Y)
,
the relation
\overset{r}{\approx}
on
[X,Y]
is an equivalence.

§ 9. Presheaves and extenders

Let

P
be a finite partially ordered set
and

C
be a concrete category.
(Concreteness is not essential;
we assume it for convenience of notation only.)
A cofunctor
S:P\to C
is called a
presheaf
.
For
p,q\in P
,
p\geqslant q
,
we have the induced morphism

{?}|_{q}:S(p)\to S(q)

(the
restriction
morphism).

For a preasheaf
U:P\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Ab}}}
,
we have the isomorphism

\nabla_{P}:\bigoplus_{p\in P}U(p)\to\bigoplus_{p\in P}U(p),\qquad{\mathrm{in}}_{p}(u)\mapsto\sum_{q\in P\lceil p\rceil}{\mathrm{in}}_{q}(u|_{q}),\qquad u\in U(p),\ p\in P.

Hereafter,

P\lceil p\rceil=\{\,q\in P\mid p\geqslant q\,\}

and

{\mathrm{in}}_{q}:U(q)\to\bigoplus_{p\in P}U(p)

are the canonical insertions.

Suppose that

P
has
the infimum operation
\wedge

and
the greatest element
\top
.
It follows that

P
is a lattice.
We put
P^{\times}=P\setminus\{\top\}
.
An
extender

\lambda
for the preasheaf
S
is a
collection of morphisms

\lambda^{q}_{p}:S(q)\to S(p),\qquad p,q\in P,\ p\geqslant q,

such that,
for

p,q\in P

and

s\in S(q)
,

\lambda^{q}_{p}(s)|_{q}=s\qquad\text{if $p\geqslant q$}

and

\lambda^{q}_{\top}(s)|_{p}=\lambda^{p\wedge q}_{p}(s|_{p\wedge q}).

In particular,

\lambda^{q}_{p}(s)=\lambda^{q}_{\top}(s)|_{p}.

(The extenders we deal with satisfy the identity

\lambda^{q}_{p}\circ\lambda^{r}_{q}=\lambda^{r}_{p}
.
We
neither check
nor use
this.)

Consider a preaheaf
U:P\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Ab}}}
with an extender
\lambda
.
The symbol
\mathbin{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.25832pt\vskip 1.24857pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\vskip 1.24857pt\hrule height=0.25832pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.25832pt\vskip 1.24857pt\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\vskip 1.24857pt\hrule height=0.25832pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.21529pt\vskip 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\vskip 1.07639pt\hrule height=0.21529pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.21529pt\vskip 0.73193pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\vskip 0.73193pt\hrule height=0.21529pt}}}}
below denotes the homomorphism of a direct
sum given by its restrictions to the summands.

For
q\in P
,
the diagram

\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{p\in P}U(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{p\in P}U(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nabla_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{p\in P}\lambda^{p}_{\top}}
\scriptstyle{R_{q}}
\textstyle{U(\top)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{q}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{p\in P\lceil q\rceil}U(p)}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{p\in P\lceil q\rceil}U(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nabla_{P\lceil q\rceil}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{p\in P\lceil q\rceil}\lambda^{p}_{q}}
\textstyle{U(q),}

where
R_{q}
is the homomorphism defined by the rule

{\mathrm{in}}_{p}(u)\mapsto{\mathrm{in}}_{p\wedge q}(u|_{p\wedge q}),

is commutative.

Direct check.
∎

The homomorphism

U(\top)\to\lim_{p\in P^{\times}}U(p),\qquad u\mapsto(u|_{p})_{p\in P^{\times}},

is surjective.

Take a collection

(u_{p})_{p\in P^{\times}}\in\lim_{p\in P^{\times}}U(p)\subseteq\bigoplus_{p\in P^{\times}}U(p).

Define
a collection
(v_{p})_{p\in P^{\times}}

and
a section
u

by the diagram

\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{p\in P^{\times}}U(p)}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{p\in P^{\times}}U(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nabla_{P^{\times}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{p\in P^{\times}}\lambda^{p}_{\top}}
\textstyle{U(\top).}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(u_{p})_{p\in P^{\times}}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(v_{p})_{p\in P^{\times}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle u}}

Take
q\in P^{\times}
.
We show that

u|_{q}=u_{q}
,
which will suffice.
In the diagram of Lemma 9.1,
we have

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(u_{p})_{p\in P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(3)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(v_{p})_{p\in P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1)}
\scriptstyle{(2)}
\scriptstyle{(4)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(5)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(u_{p})_{p\in P\lceil q\rceil}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{in}}_{q}(u_{q})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(6)}
\scriptstyle{(7)}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle u_{q},}}

where we put
u_{\top}=v_{\top}=0
in
U(\top)
.
The sendings
(1)
and
(2)
follow from the construction of
the collections.
The sending
(6)
expresses the equalities
u_{q}|_{p}=u_{p}
,

p\in P\lceil q\rceil
,
which hold by the definition of limit.
The sending
(3)
is obvious.
The sending
(4)
follows because
the left square is commutative
and

\nabla_{P\lceil q\rceil}
is injective.
The sending
(7)
is the equality
\lambda^{q}_{q}={\mathrm{id}}
,
which follows from the definition of extender.
By commutativity of the right square,
the sending
(5)
holds,
which is what was to be checked.
∎

§ 10. The abstract fissilizer
\Phi_{E}
on
\langle\underline{M}(E)\rangle

Fix a nonempty finite set
E
.
The set
{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)
is partially ordered by inclusion.

For
A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
we say
A\geqslant B
if,
for each
G\in B
,
there is
F\in A
such that

F\supseteq G
.
Such an
F
is unique;
we denote it by
(A)G
.
The set
{\mathscr{A}}(E)
becomes a lattice with
the infimum operation

A\wedge B=\{\,F\cap G\mid F\in A,\ G\in B\,\}\setminus\{\varnothing\}

and
the greatest element
\top=\{E\}
.

Let

{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Mg}}}
be the category of sets
and

M:{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Mg}}}
be a presheaf.
We define a presheaf
\underline{M}:{\mathscr{A}}(E)\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Mg}}}
.
For
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
put

\underline{M}(A)=\prod_{F\in A}M(F).

For
A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
A\geqslant B
,
define the restriction function

\underline{M}(A)\to\underline{M}(B),\qquad\underline{m}\mapsto\underline{m}|_{B},

by putting,
for
\underline{m}=(m_{F})_{F\in A}
,

\underline{m}|_{B}=(m_{(A)G}|_{G})_{G\in B}.

Clearly,

\underline{M}(\{E\})=M(E)
.

Taking composition with the functor
\langle{?}\rangle:{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Mg}}}\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Ab}}}
,
we get the presheaves

{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Ab}}},\qquad F\mapsto\langle M(F)\rangle,

and

{\mathscr{A}}(E)\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Ab}}},\qquad A\mapsto\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle.

(6)

For
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
we have the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-multilinear operation

\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}:\prod_{F\in A}\langle M(F)\rangle\to\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle,\qquad\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}m_{F}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}(m_{F})_{F\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}

(7)

(cf. § 2).
For

Q\in\langle M(E)\rangle

and

A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
put

Q^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}(A)=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}Q|_{F}\in\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle.

We call an ensemble
R\in\langle M(E)\rangle

fissile
if,
for any layout
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,

R|_{A}=R^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}(A)

in
\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle
.

We suppose that
the presheaf
\underline{M}
has an extender

\lambda^{B}_{A}:\underline{M}(B)\to\underline{M}(A),\qquad A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E),\ A\geqslant B.

Then
the preasheaf (
6
) has the extender

\langle\lambda^{B}_{A}\rangle:\langle\underline{M}(B)\rangle\to\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle,\qquad A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E),\ A\geqslant B.

For
Q\in\langle M(E)\rangle
,
define an ensemble
\Phi_{E}(Q)\in\langle M(E)\rangle

by the rule

\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\lambda^{A}_{\{E\}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle M(E)\rangle.}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle Q^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)}^{-1}(Q^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

We get a function
(not a homomorphism)

\Phi_{E}:\langle M(E)\rangle\to\langle M(E)\rangle,

which we call the
fissilizer
.

For any ensemble
Q\in\langle M(E)\rangle
,
the ensemble
\Phi_{E}(Q)
is fissile.

Take
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
.
We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle Q^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)}^{-1}(Q^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\Phi_{E}(Q)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{a\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\lambda^{a}_{\{E\}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{R_{A}}
\textstyle{\langle M(E)\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{A}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{a\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)\lceil A\rceil}\langle\underline{M}(a)\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J_{A}}

\scriptstyle{\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)\lceil A\rceil}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{a\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)\lceil A\rceil}\langle\lambda^{a}_{A}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{J_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\bigotimes\limits_{F\in A}\ \bigoplus\limits_{b\in{\mathscr{A}}(F)}\langle\underline{M}(b)\rangle}
\textstyle{\bigotimes\limits_{F\in A}\ \bigoplus\limits_{b\in{\mathscr{A}}(F)}\langle\underline{M}(b)\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\bigotimes\limits_{F\in A}\Phi_{F}(Q|_{F})}}

where
the upper half comes from Lemma 9.1,

I_{A}
is the isomorphism defined by the rule

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}(m_{F})_{F\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}\mapsto\bigotimes_{F\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}m_{F}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}},

and

J_{A}
is the isomorphism defined by the rule

{\mathrm{in}}_{a}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\underline{m}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})\mapsto\bigotimes_{F\in A}{\mathrm{in}}_{a\wedge\{F\}}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\underline{m}|_{a\wedge\{F\}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})

(note that

a\wedge\{F\}\in{\mathscr{A}}(F)\subseteq{\mathscr{A}}(E)
).
Commutativity of the lower half is checked directly.
The sendings in the upper row hold
by the definition of
\Phi_{E}
.
The sendings in the lower row hold
by the definition of
\Phi_{F}:\langle M(F)\rangle\to\langle M(F)\rangle
.
The sending in the left column is checked directly.
The sending in the right column follows.
Since

I_{A}:\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}q_{F}\mapsto\bigotimes_{F\in A}q_{F}

for
q_{F}\in\langle M(F)\rangle
,

F\in A
,
we get

\Phi_{E}(Q)|_{A}=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}\Phi_{F}(Q|_{F}).

In particular,
for
A=\{F\}
,
this gives

\Phi_{E}(Q)|_{F}=\Phi_{F}(Q|_{F}).

Thus,
for arbitrary
A
,

\Phi_{E}(Q)|_{A}=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}\Phi_{E}(Q)|_{F}.

Thus

\Phi_{E}(Q)
is fissile.
∎

Let
N(A)\subseteq\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle
,
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
, be a collection
of subgroups preserved by
the restriction homomorphisms
and
the homomorphisms
\langle\lambda^{B}_{A}\rangle
.

Let an ensemble
Q\in\langle M(E)\rangle
satisfy

Q^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.45206pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}(A)-Q|_{A}\in N(A)

for all
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
.
Then

\Phi_{E}(Q)-Q\in N(\{E\}).

We have the presheaf

{\mathscr{A}}(E)\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Ab}}},\qquad A\mapsto\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle/N(A),

with the induced restriction homomorphisms.
We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle(Q|_{A})_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{in}}_{\{E\}}(Q)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle Q}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle Q^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}}}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)}^{-1}(Q^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.32289pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\Phi_{E}(Q)}}
