\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigoplus$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\lambda^{A}_{\{E\}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}}
\textstyle{\langle M(E)\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle/N(A)}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)}\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle/N(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\langle M(E)\rangle/N(\{E\}).}

The upper line of sendings is obvious.
The lower line of sendings holds by the definition of

\Phi_{E}
.
By hypothesis,
the difference of the elements in the upper-left corner
descends to zero.
Since
\nabla_{{\mathscr{A}}(E)}
in the lower row is an isomorphism,
the difference of elements in the upper-right corner
also descends to zero.
∎

§ 11. Topological and simplicial constructions

Topological cones.

Take
s\in\{0,1\}
.
Given an unpointed space
U
,
form the space

{\mathrm{C}}^{s}U=(U\times[0,1])/(U\times\{s\}),

the
cone
over
U
.
The innate basepoint
(where
U\times\{s\}
is projected)
is called the
apex
.
Using the “base” embedding

U\xrightarrow{u\mapsto(u,1-s)}U\times[0,1]\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{pr}}}{\mathrm{C}}^{s}U,

we adopt
the inclusion
U\subseteq{\mathrm{C}}^{s}U

and
the based one
U_{+}\subseteq{\mathrm{C}}^{s}U
.
A path of the form

[0,1]\xrightarrow{t\mapsto(u,t)}U\times[0,1]\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{pr}}}{\mathrm{C}}^{s}U

is called a
generating path
.
For an unpointed subspace
V\subseteq U
,
we have
{\mathrm{C}}^{s}V\subseteq{\mathrm{C}}^{s}U
.

Notation:

{\check{\mathrm{C}}}={\mathrm{C}}^{0}
,

{\hat{\mathrm{C}}}={\mathrm{C}}^{1}
.

Topological suspensions.

For an unpointed space
U
,
the
unreduced suspension

{\overline{\Sigma}}U
is the colimit
of the diagram

\{0,1\}\xleftarrow{{\mathrm{pr}}}U\times\{0,1\}\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{in}}}U\times[0,1].

Let
s_{{\overline{\Sigma}}U}\in{\overline{\Sigma}}U
be the point coming from

s\in\{0,1\}
.
We appoint
0_{{\overline{\Sigma}}U}
to be the basepoint of
{\overline{\Sigma}}U
.

We use also the usual reduced suspension
\Sigma
.

Simplicial notation.

The simplex category consists of the sets

[n]=\{0,\dotsc,n\}
.
Let

{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}_{[n]}
denote the
n
th term of a simplicial set

\textstyle\sib U

and

{\sib f}_{[n]}:{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}_{[n]}\to{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib V$}}}_{[n]}
be the
n
th term of a morphism

{\sib f}:{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}\to{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib V$}}}
.
Let
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{n}
be “the
n
-simplex”,
the simplicial set represented
(as a cofunctor)
by the object
[n]
.

Unreduced Kan cones.

Take
s\in\{0,1\}
.
Let
{\svb d}^{s}:{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{0}\to{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{1}
be the morphism induced by the
function
\delta^{s}:[0]\to[1]
,

0\mapsto 1-s
.
Given a simplicial set

\textstyle\sib U

,
we define its
cone

{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
.
There is a unique
(up to an isomorphism)
Cartesian square

\textstyle\sib U

\scriptstyle{{\sib i}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sib p}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\svb d}^{s}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{1}}

with the universal property expressed by the diagram

\textstyle\sib U

\scriptstyle{{\sib i}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sib p}}

\textstyle\sib A

\textstyle\sib B

\textstyle{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\svb d}^{s}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{1},}

where the lower trapeze is assumed to be Cartesian.
The morphism
{\svb d}^{1-s}:{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{0}\to{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{1}
lifts along
{\sib p}

uniquely.
This yields a morphism
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{0}\to{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
,
which makes
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
a pointed simplicial set.
The basepoint is called the
apex
.
The morphism
{\sib i}
is injective.
Using it,
we adopt
the inclusion
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}

and
the based one
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}_{+}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
.
We call
{\sib p}
the
projection
.

All constructions are covariant/natural in

\textstyle\sib U

.
The functor
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}
preserves injective morphisms.
Using this,
we adopt the inclusion
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib V$}}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}

for a simplicial subset
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib V$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib V$}}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
.

Notation:

{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}={\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{0}
,

{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}={\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{1}
.

There is a unique natural map
r:{\mathrm{C}}^{s}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|\to|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|
such that
the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathrm{C}}^{s}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\textstyle{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
{\mathrm{in}}
(
=|{\sib i}|
)
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{in}}}
\textstyle{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|}

is commutative
and
each generating path of
{\mathrm{C}}^{s}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|
is sent to an affine path
in some simplex of
|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|
.
The map
r
is a homeomorphism.
Using it,
we adopt that

|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}^{s}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|={\mathrm{C}}^{s}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|
.

The reduced Kan cone.

For a pointed simplicial set

\textstyle\sib T

,
introduce the pointed simplicial set

{\boldsymbol{\check{\sibr c}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib T$}}}={\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib T$}}}/{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}})
,
where
({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}})\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib T$}}}
is the simplicial subset
generated by the basepoint
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\in{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib T$}}}_{[0]}

(so,

({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}})\cong{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Delta$}}}^{0}
).
We adopt the obvious
inclusion
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib T$}}}\subseteq{\boldsymbol{\check{\sibr c}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib T$}}}

and
identification
{\boldsymbol{\check{\sibr c}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}_{+})={\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
.

{\boldsymbol{\check{\sibr c}}}
is a functor;
it preserves wedges.

The unreduced Kan suspension.

For a simplicial set

\textstyle\sib U

,
introduce the pointed simplicial set

{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}={\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}/{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
.
It has two vertices:
the top
1_{{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}}
,
which is the image
of the apex of the cone
{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}

under the projection
{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}\to{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
,
and
the basepoint
0_{{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}}

(where the base
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}\subseteq{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}
is sent).
We have

|{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|=|{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|/|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|={\hat{\mathrm{C}}}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|/|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|={\overline{\Sigma}}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib U$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib U$}}}|.

The thick simplex.

For a set
U
,
let
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U
be the simplicial set with

({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U)_{[n]}=U^{[n]}

(
=U^{n+1}
)
and obvious structure functions.

For each
u\in U
,
there is a unique retraction
\widetilde{\svb s}_{u}:{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U\to{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U

sending
the apex
to the vertex
u\in U=({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U)_{[0]}\subseteq({\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U)_{[0]}
.
Define retractions

\overline{{\svb s}}_{u}

and

{\svb s}_{u}

by the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\sib q}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\svb s}_{u}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sib r}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{{\svb s}}_{u}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\sibr c}}}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\svb s}_{u}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sib q}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U,}

where
{\sib q}
and
{\sib r}
are projections.
We call
{\svb s}_{u}
the
canonical contraction
.

Let

V\subseteq U
be a subset.
Then,
for
u\in V
,
the diagram

\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\sibr c}}}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\svb s}_{u}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\sibr c}}}{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\svb s}_{u}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr\Sigma$}}}}}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U,}

where the horizontal arrows are induced by the inclusion
V\to U
,
is commutative.
∎

If
U
is finite,
let

\xi_{U}:|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U|\to\Delta U

(8)

be the unbased map
that sends,
for each
u\in U
,
the corresponding vertex
|u|
of
|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr E$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr E$}}}U|
to
the corresponding vertex
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}u{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}
of
\Delta U

and
is affine on simplices.
Hereafter,
we put
\Delta\varnothing=\varnothing
.

Barycentric subdivision.

Let

\textstyle\si K

be an
(abstract simplicial)
complex.
We order the set of simplices of

\textstyle\si K

by reverse inclusion.
Define the simplicial set
\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}
as
the nerve of this partially ordered set.
For a subcomplex
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}
,
we have
\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}\subseteq\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}
.

There is a homeomorphism
|\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}|\to|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}|
that
sends
the vertex of
|\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}|

corresponding to a simplex
k
of

\textstyle\si K

to
the barycentre of the simplex
|k|\subseteq|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}|

and
takes
each simplex of
|\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}|

to
some simplex of
|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}|

affinely.
Using it,
we adopt that

|\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}|=|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}|
.

Canonical retractions.

Given
a complex

\textstyle\si K

and
a subcomplex
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}
,
we
have
{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}\subseteq{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}

and
define the based morphism

{\svb r}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}:{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}\to{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}

as the retraction
that sends all vertices outside
{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}
to the apex of

{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}
.
We call
{\svb r}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}
the
canonical retraction
.

For two subcomplexes
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si M$}}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}
,
the diagram

\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sibr{in}}}
\scriptstyle{{\svb r}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}\cap{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si M$}}}}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\svb r}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si K$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si K$}}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si M$}}}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si L$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si L$}}}\cap{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si M$}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sibr{in}}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\si M$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\si M$}}}}

is commutative.
∎

§ 12. Canonical retractions in the cones
{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E

and
{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E

Fix a nonempty finite set
E
.

The simplex
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E
and its subcomplexes.

Let the
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E
be the complex whose
set of vertices is
E

and
set of simplices is
{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)
.
For
F\in{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)
,
we have the subcomplex
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}F\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E
.
For
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
introduce the subcomplex
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]\subseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E
,

{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]=\bigcup_{F\in A}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}F.

For
A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
we have

A\geqslant B\ \Rightarrow\ {\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]\supseteq{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[B]

and

{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A\wedge B]={\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]\cap{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[B]
.
Moreover,

{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[\{E\}]={\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E
.

For
A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
A\geqslant B
,
we have
the canonical retraction

{\svb r}^{A}_{B}={\svb r}^{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[B]}:{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]\to{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[B].

For two layouts
A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
the diagram

\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sibr{in}}}
\scriptstyle{{\svb r}^{A}_{A\wedge B}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\svb r}^{\{E\}}_{B}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A\wedge B]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sibr{in}}}
\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[B]}

is commutative.

Follows from Lemma 11.2.
∎

Geometric realization.

We adopt the obvious identification
|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E|=\Delta E
.
For
F\in{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)
,

|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}F|=\Delta F
as subsets of
\Delta E
.
For
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,

|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]|=\Delta[A]
in the same sense.
For
A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
A\geqslant B
,
we have

\Delta[A]\supseteq\Delta[B]

and
the retraction
\rho^{A}_{B}
,

\textstyle{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{A}_{B}}
\textstyle{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[B]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{|{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]|\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{|{\svb r}^{A}_{B}|}
\textstyle{|{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[B]|.}

We call
\rho^{A}_{B}
the
canonical retraction
,
too.

For two layouts
A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
the diagram

\textstyle{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{in}}}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{A}_{A\wedge B}}
\textstyle{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\{E\}}_{B}}
\textstyle{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A\wedge B]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{in}}}
\textstyle{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[B]}

is commutative.

Follows from Corollary 12.1.
∎

§ 13. The fissilizer
\Phi_{E}
on

\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle

Fix
a space
Z

and
a finite set
E
.
Consider the presheaf
M:{\mathscr{P}}_{\times}(E)\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Mg}}}
,

F\mapsto Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F}

(with the obvious restriction functions).
For
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
we have

{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]={\check{\mathrm{C}}}\bigl(\bigcup_{F\in A}\Delta F\bigr)=\bigvee_{F\in A}{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F.

We identify the presheaf
\underline{M}:{\mathscr{A}}(E)\to{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Mg}}}

(see § 10)
with the presheaf
A\mapsto Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}

by the chain of equalities/obvious identifications

\underline{M}(A)=\prod_{F\in A}M(F)=\prod_{F\in A}Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F}=Z^{\bigvee_{F\in A}{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F}=Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}.

The operation (
7
) in our case coincides with the operation

\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}:\prod_{F\in A}\langle Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F}\rangle\to\langle Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle,

which we have by § 2.
We will need the following formulas:

\epsilon\bigl(\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}Q_{F}\bigr)=\prod_{F\in A}\epsilon(Q_{F})

(9)

and

\bigl(\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}Q_{F}\bigr)\big|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G}=\bigl(\prod_{F\in A\setminus\{G\}}\epsilon(Q_{F})\bigr)Q_{G}.

(10)

For
A,B\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
A\geqslant B
,
let
\lambda^{B}_{A}:\underline{M}(B)\to\underline{M}(A)
be the function

Z^{\rho^{A}_{B}}:Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[B]}\to Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]},

where

\rho^{A}_{B}:{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]\to{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[B]
is
the canonical retraction.
It follows from Corollary 12.2 that
the functions
\lambda^{B}_{A}
form an extender.
By § 10,
we get the fissilizer

\Phi_{E}:\langle Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle\to\langle Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle.

For any ensemble
Q\in\langle Z^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle
,
the ensemble
\Phi_{E}(Q)
is fissile.

Follows from Lemma 10.1.
∎

We set
Z=Y^{X}_{a}
,
where

X
and
Y
are spaces,

X
compact Hausdorff,
and

a:X\to Y
is a map.
For a space
T
,
we have the inclusion
\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{T}\rangle\subseteq\langle(Y^{X},a)^{T}\rangle
.

An ensemble
Q\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle
is called

(X,r)
-almost fissile
if,
for any layout
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,

\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F}-Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

Any affine ensemble
Q\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle
is

(X,1)
-almost fissile.

Take
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
.
Consider the quantity
D\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle
,

D=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F}-Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}.

We should show that

D\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle^{(2)}_{X}
.
Consider the homomorphism

\langle\square^{X}\rangle:\langle(Y^{X},a)^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle\to\langle Y^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]\times X}\rangle.

We should show that

\langle\square^{X}\rangle(D)\in\langle Y^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]\times X}\rangle^{(2)}
.
Take
R\in{\mathscr{F}}_{1}({\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]\times X)
.
We check that

\langle\square^{X}\rangle(D)|_{R}=0
.
We are in (at least) one of the two following cases.

Case 0:

R=\{{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\}
.
We have

\epsilon(\langle\square^{X}\rangle(D))=\epsilon(D)=\hfill\text{(using \eqref{epsilon-hash})}\hfill=\prod_{F\in A}\epsilon(Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F})-\epsilon(Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]})=\qquad\\
\qquad=\prod_{F\in A}\epsilon(Q)-\epsilon(Q)=\hfill\text{(since $\epsilon(Q)=1$)}\hfill=0,

which suffices in this case.

Case 1:

R\subseteq{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G\times X

for some
G\in A
.
It suffices to check that

\langle\square^{X}\rangle(D)|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G\times X}=0
.
We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X},a)^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G}}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]\times X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{?}|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G\times X}}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X},a)^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G\times X}\rangle.}

Thus
it suffices to check that

D|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G}=0
.
We have

D|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G}=\hfill\text{(using \eqref{hash-restricted})}\hfill=\bigl(\prod_{F\in A\setminus\{G\}}\epsilon(Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F})\bigr)Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G}-Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta G}=\qquad\\
\qquad\text{(since $\epsilon(Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F})=\epsilon(Q)=1$)}\hfill=0.\displaymath@qed

Let
Q\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle
be an
(X,r)
-almost
fissile ensemble.
Then

\Phi_{E}(Q)-Q\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

For
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
introduce the subgroup

N(A)=\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle\cap\langle(Y^{X},a)^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}\subseteq\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle=\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle.

By Lemma 3.1,
this family is preserved by
the restriction homomorphisms of the presheaf

A\mapsto\langle\underline{M}(A)\rangle

and
the homomorphisms
\langle\lambda^{B}_{A}\rangle
.
Since the ensemble
Q
is
(X,r)
-almost fissile,
it satisfies the hypothesis of Lemma 10.2.
Thus

\Phi_{E}(Q)-Q\in N(\{E\})
,
as required.
∎

Suppose that

X

and

Y

are cellular.
Given maps
a,b:X\to Y
,
let us say that

a
is
firmly
r
-similar to
b
,

a\overset{r}{\approxident}b
,
if,
for any nonempty finite set
E
,
there is a fissile ensemble
R\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle

such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-R|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

(11)

Let
a,b:X\to Y
be maps.
Then

a\overset{r}{\approxident}b
implies
a\overset{r}{\approx}b
.

We do not know whether the converse holds.

Take a nonempty finite set
E
.
We have a fissile ensemble
R\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle

satisfying (
11
).
We seek a fissile ensemble
S\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle

such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-S\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

(12)

Put
S=R|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}
.

For a layout
A\in{\mathscr{A}}(E)
,
we have

S|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}=R|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}=\hfill\text{(since $R$ is fissile)}\hfill=\bigl(\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}R|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F}\bigr)\big|_{\Delta[A]_{+}}=\qquad\\
\qquad\text{(by naturality of $\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sbighash$}}}$)}\hfill=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}R|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}=\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}S|_{(\Delta F)_{+}}.

Thus
S
is fissile.

The condition (
12
) is just the equality (
11
).
∎

Let
a,b:X\to Y
be maps.
Suppose that,
for any finite set
E
,
there is an
(X,r)
-almost fissile ensemble

Q_{E}\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle
such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-Q_{E}|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

(13)

Then

a\overset{r}{\approx}b

and,
moreover,

a\overset{r}{\approxident}b
.

Take a nonempty finite set
E
.
Put

Q=Q_{E}

and

R=\Phi_{E}(Q)\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle
.
By Corollary 13.1,

R
is fissile.
By Corollary 13.3,

R-Q\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

By Lemma 3.1,

R|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}-Q|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

Using (
13
),
we get

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-R|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

Thus

a\overset{r}{\approxident}b
.
By Lemma 13.4,

a\overset{r}{\approx}b
.
∎

§ 14. Strong
1
-similarity

Let

X

and

Y

be spaces,

X
compact Hausdorff,
and

a:X\to Y
be a map.

Let
U
be an unpointed space.
Then
the homomorphism

\langle\theta^{U}_{a}\rangle:\langle Y^{X}\rangle\to\langle(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\rangle

takes
\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(s)}
to
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\rangle^{(s)}_{X}
.

Introduce the map

p:U_{+}\times X\to X\vee X,\qquad(u,x)\mapsto{\mathrm{in}}_{1}(x),\ ({\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}},x)\mapsto{\mathrm{in}}_{2}(x).

(
u\in U
,

x\in X
).
We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\langle Y^{X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\theta^{U}_{a}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\langle e\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\square^{X}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{X\vee X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle Y^{p}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle Y^{U_{+}\times X}\rangle,}

where
e={?}\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}a:Y^{X}\to Y^{X\vee X}
.

We show that

\langle e\rangle
sends
\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(s)}
to
\langle Y^{X\vee X}\rangle^{(s)}
.
Indeed,

\langle e\rangle
equals the composition

\langle Y^{X}\rangle\xrightarrow{{?}\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.1507pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}a{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.3014pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.1507pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\langle Y^{X}\rangle\otimes\langle Y^{X}\rangle\xrightarrow{{({\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}})}}\langle Y^{X\vee X}\rangle

(see
[
3
, § 3]
for
{({\mathbin{\overline{\underline{\vee}}}})}
).
Here

\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(s)}
goes to
\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(s)}\otimes\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(0)}
,
which goes to
\langle Y^{X\vee X}\rangle^{(s)}

by
[
3
, Lemma 3.1]
.

The homomorphism
\langle Y^{p}\rangle
takes
\langle Y^{X\vee X}\rangle^{(s)}
to

\langle Y^{U_{+}\times X}\rangle^{(s)}

by
[
3
, Lemma 2.1]
.
Thus,
by the diagram,

\langle\theta^{U}_{a}\rangle
takes
\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(s)}
to

\langle\square^{X}\rangle^{-1}(\langle Y^{U_{+}\times X}\rangle^{(s)})
,
which is
\langle(Y^{X},a)^{U_{+}}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}

by definition.
∎

Suppose that

X

and

Y

are cellular.

Let
a,b:X\to Y
be maps such that

a\overset{1}{\sim}b
.
Then

a\overset{1}{\approx}b
.

We have an ensemble
A\in\langle Y^{X}_{a}\rangle
,

A=\sum_{i}u_{i}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}a_{i}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}},

such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}b{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-A\in\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(2)}
.
For each
i
,
choose a path
h_{i}:[0,1]\to Y^{X}_{a}
from
a
to
a_{i}

and
consider the composition

q_{i}:{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E\xrightarrow{\mathrm{projection}}[0,1]\xrightarrow{h_{i}}Y^{X}_{a}.

Consider the ensemble
Q\in\langle(Y^{X}_{a})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle
,

Q=\sum_{i}u_{i}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}q_{i}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}.

We have

\epsilon(Q)=\epsilon(A)=\hfill\text{(since ${\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}b{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-A\in\langle Y^{X}\rangle^{(1)}$)}\hfill=\epsilon({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}b{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})=1.

By Lemma 13.2,

Q
is
(X,1)
-almost fissile.
Clearly,

q_{i}|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}=\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(a_{i})
.
Thus

Q|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}=\langle\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}\rangle(A)
.
We get

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}(b){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-Q|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}=\langle\theta^{\Delta E}_{a}\rangle({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}b{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-A)\in\langle(Y^{X},a)^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(2)},

where
\in
holds by Lemma 14.1.
By Proposition 13.5,

a\overset{1}{\approx}b
.
∎

§ 15. Two identities

Let

A

and

I

be finite sets.
Let
{\mathscr{P}}(I)
be the set of subsets of
I
.
Consider the set
{\mathscr{P}}(I)^{A}
of functions
k:A\to{\mathscr{P}}(I)
.
For
k\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)^{A}
,
put

U(k)=\bigcup_{a\in A}k(a)\in{\mathscr{P}}(I).

Let
{\mathscr{R}}(A,I)
be the set of
k\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)^{A}
such that

U(k)=I

(covers).

In the group
\langle{\mathscr{P}}(I)\rangle^{\otimes A}
,
the equality holds

\sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)}(-1)^{|I|-|J|}\bigotimes_{a\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}=\sum_{k\in{\mathscr{R}}(A,I)}\ \bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(k(a))}(-1)^{|k(a)|-|J|}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}.

We have

\sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)}(-1)^{|I|-|J|}\bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{K\in{\mathscr{P}}(J)}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}K{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}=\\
=\sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)}(-1)^{|I|-|J|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)^{A}:\\
U(k)\subseteq J\end{subarray}}\ \bigotimes_{a\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}k(a){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}=\\
=\sum_{k\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)^{A}}\bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in{\mathscr{P}}(I):\\
J\supseteq U(k)\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|-|J|}\bigr)\bigotimes_{a\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}k(a){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}\overset{(*)}{=}\sum_{k\in{\mathscr{R}}(A,I)}\ \bigotimes_{a\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}k(a){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}},

where
(*)
holds because
the inner sum on the left equals

1

if
U(k)=I

and

0

otherwise.
The set
{\mathscr{P}}(I)
is partially ordered by inclusion.
We have the isomorphism

\nabla_{{\mathscr{P}}(I)}^{-1}:\langle{\mathscr{P}}(I)\rangle\to\langle{\mathscr{P}}(I)\rangle

(see § 9),
under which

\sum_{K\in{\mathscr{P}}(J)}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}K{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}\mapsto{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}},\qquad J\in{\mathscr{P}}(I),

and

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}K{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}\mapsto\sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(K)}(-1)^{|K|-|J|}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}},\qquad K\in{\mathscr{P}}(I).

Applying it to each factor of the summands in the
left
and
right
sides of the calculation,
we get the required equality.
∎

Put
{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)={\mathscr{P}}(I)\setminus\{I\}
.
We adopt the inclusion
{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)^{A}\subseteq{\mathscr{P}}(I)^{A}
.
Let
{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}(A,I)
be the set of
k\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)^{A}
such that

U(k)=I
.

In the group
\langle{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)\rangle^{\otimes A}
,
the equality holds

\bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)}(-1)^{|I|-1-|J|}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-\sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)}(-1)^{|I|-1-|J|}\bigotimes_{a\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}=\\
=\sum_{k\in{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}(A,I)}\ \bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(k(a))}(-1)^{|k(a)|-|J|}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}.

We use the inclusion

\langle{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)\rangle^{\otimes A}\subseteq\langle{\mathscr{P}}(I)\rangle^{\otimes A}
.
Put

T(k)=\bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(k(a))}(-1)^{|k(a)|-|J|}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}},\qquad k\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)^{A}.

We have

\sum_{k\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)^{A}}T(k)=\bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{K\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)}\ \sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(K)}(-1)^{|K|-|J|}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}=\\
=\bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)}\bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\in{\mathscr{P}}(I):\\
K\supseteq J\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|K|-|J|}\bigr){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}\overset{(*)}{=}\bigotimes_{a\in A}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}I{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}},

(14)

where
(*)
holds because
the inner sum on the left equals

1

if
J=I

and

0

otherwise.
We have also

\sum_{k\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)^{A}}T(k)=\bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{K\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)}\ \sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}(K)}(-1)^{|K|-|J|}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}J{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}=\\
=\bigotimes_{a\in A}\ \sum_{J\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)}\bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I):\\
