is
r
-almost fissile,

\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}q|_{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}F}-q|_{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]}\in\langle{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib W$}}}(I)^{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}[A]}\rangle^{[r+1]}.

By Lemma 22.2,

\mathop{\sbighash}_{F\in A}Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta F}-Q|_{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\in\langle(Y^{X})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta[A]}\rangle^{(r+1)}_{X}.

Thus
Q
is
(X,r)
-almost fissile.
∎

§ 23. Brunnian loops in a wedge of circles

Fix a finite set
I
of cardinality
s
.
Put

B(I)=I_{+}\wedge S^{1}

(a wedge of
s
circles).
Let the monoid
{\mathscr{P}}(I)
act on the space
I_{+}
by putting

J_{(I_{+})}(i)=\begin{cases}i&\text{if $i\in J$,}\\
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

for

i\in I_{+}
,

J\in{\mathscr{P}}(I)
.
This action induces one on
B(I)
.
A map
v:S^{1}\to B(I)

(a loop)
is called
Brunnian
if
the composition

S^{1}\xrightarrow{v}B(I)\xrightarrow{J_{(B(I))}}B(I)

is null-homotopic
for all
J\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)
.

Let
w:S^{1}\to B(I)
be a Brunnian loop.
Then
{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{s-1}{\approx}w
.

Take a finite set
E
.
Consider the loop space
B(I)^{S^{1}}
.
It carries the induced action of the monoid
{\mathscr{P}}(I)
.
The path component
B(I)^{S^{1}}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}
is weakly contractible.
Since
w
is Brunnian,

J_{(B(I)^{S^{1}})}(w)

(
=J_{(B(I))}\circ w)

\in B(I)^{S^{1}}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}

for all
J\in{\mathscr{P}}^{\times}(I)
.
Therefore,
Lemma 21.1 yields
a
{\mathscr{P}}(I)
-equivariant map
h:V(I)\to B(I)^{S^{1}}
such that

h(\top_{V(I)})=w
.
We get the realization homomorphism

\langle\Upsilon_{h}\rangle:\langle{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib W$}}}(I)^{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E}\rangle\to\langle(B(I)^{S^{1}})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle.

By Corollary 19.4,
there is an
(s-1)
-almost fissile ensemble

q\in\langle{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib W$}}}^{\times}(I)^{{\boldsymbol{\check{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sibr C$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sibr C$}}}}}\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E}\rangle
such that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}{\svb j}^{E}_{I}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-q|_{(\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E)_{+}}\in\langle{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib W$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib W$}}}(I)^{(\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E)_{+}}\rangle^{[s]}.

(26)

Put
Q=\langle\Upsilon_{h}\rangle(q)
.
By Lemma 22.1,

Q\in\langle(B(I)^{S^{1}}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}})^{{\check{\mathrm{C}}}\Delta E}\rangle
.
By Lemma 22.3,

Q
is
({S^{1}},s-1)
-almost fissile.
We have

\Upsilon_{h}({\svb j}^{E}_{I})=\hfill\text{(by construction of $\Upsilon_{h}$)}\hfill=\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(h(\top_{V(I)}))=\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(w)

and

Q|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}=\langle\Upsilon_{h}\rangle(q)|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}=\\
\qquad\text{(by naturality of $\Upsilon^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib T$}}}_{h}$ with respect to ${\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sib T$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sib T$}}}$)}\hfill=\langle\Upsilon_{h}\rangle(q|_{(\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E)_{+}}).

Thus

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\theta^{\Delta E}_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}(w){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-Q|_{(\Delta E)_{+}}=\langle\Upsilon_{h}\rangle({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}{\svb j}^{E}_{I}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}-q|_{(\b{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.21}{$\displaystyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\textstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}{\scalebox{1.21}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sir\Delta$}}}E)_{+}})\in\langle(B(I)^{S^{1}})^{(\Delta E)_{+}}\rangle^{(s)}_{S^{1}},

where
\in
follows from (
26
)
by Lemma 22.2.
By Proposition 13.5,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{s-1}{\approx}w
.
∎

§ 24. Loops in an arbitrary space

Nested commutators.

A
nesting

t
of
weight

|t|\geqslant 1
is
either
the atom
\bullet

if
|t|=1
,
or
a pair
(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
of nestings with
|t^{\prime}|+|t^{\prime\prime}|=|t|
.
Given
elements
g_{1},\dotsc,g_{s}
of a group
G

and
a nesting
t
of weight
s
,
the
t
-nested commutator

\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}g_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}\in G

is defined
(by induction on
s
)
to be
either

g_{1}

if
s=1
,
or
the commutator

\llbracket\,\prescript{t^{\prime}\!}{}{\llbracket}g_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{|t^{\prime}|},\,\prescript{t^{\prime\prime}\!}{}{\llbracket}g_{i}\rrbracket_{i=|t^{\prime}|+1}^{s}\,\rrbracket

if
t=(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
.
The nested commutators of weight
s
in
G
generate

\gamma^{s}G
,
the
s
th term of the lower central series of
G
.

Loops.

Let
Y
be a cellular space.
We consider the group
\pi_{1}(Y)=[S^{1},Y]

with the filtration
\pi_{1}(Y)^{((s))}=[S^{1},Y]^{((s))}

(see § 8).

One has

\pi_{1}(Y)^{((s))}=\gamma^{s}\pi_{1}(Y).

Recall
[
3
, Theorem 11.2]
:

\pi_{1}(Y)^{(s)}=\gamma^{s}\pi_{1}(Y).

(27)

Thus,
by
Theorem 8.2
and

[
3
, Theorem 4.2]
,
the strong
r
-similarity on
\pi_{1}(Y)

coincides with the
r
-similarity.

The inclusion

\pi_{1}(Y)^{((s))}\subseteq\gamma^{s}\pi_{1}(Y)
follows from
the inclusion
\pi_{1}(Y)^{((s))}\subseteq\pi_{1}(Y)^{(s)}

(immediate from the definitions)
and
(
27
).

Check that

\gamma^{s}\pi_{1}(Y)\subseteq\pi_{1}(Y)^{((s))}
.
Since
\pi_{1}(Y)^{((s))}
is a subgroup
(by Theorem 8.1),
it suffices to show that,
for any
nesting
t
of weight
s

and
loops
a_{1},\dotsc,a_{s}:S^{1}\to Y
,
one has

\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[a_{i}]\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}\in\pi_{1}(Y)^{((s))}.

Put

B_{s}=\bigvee_{i\in(s)}S^{1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad a=\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.86108pt\vskip 0.99025pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.99025pt\hrule height=0.86108pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.6458pt\vskip 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 1.07639pt\hrule height=0.6458pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.55974pt\vskip 0.90419pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.90419pt\hrule height=0.55974pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.38748pt\vskip 0.6458pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.6458pt\hrule height=0.38748pt}}}}_{i\in(s)}a_{i}:B_{s}\to Y.

Let
{\mathrm{in}}_{i}:S^{1}\to B_{s}
be the canonical insertions.
Choose a loop
e:S^{1}\to B_{s}
with

[e]=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[{\mathrm{in}}_{i}]\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}

in
\pi_{1}(B_{s})
.
So
\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[a_{i}]\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}=[a\circ e]
.
Clearly,
the loop
e
is Brunnian.
By Lemma 23.1,

[e]\in\pi_{1}(B_{s})^{((s))}
.
By Corollary 5.2,

[a\circ e]\in\pi_{1}(Y)^{((s))}
,
as was to be shown.
∎

§ 25. Whitehead products

Let
T_{i}
,

i=1,2
,
be compact cellular spaces
and

T_{i}\xleftarrow{p_{i}}T_{1}\times T_{2}\xrightarrow{k}T_{1}\wedge T_{2}

be the projections.
The map

\Sigma(T_{1}\times T_{2})\xrightarrow{\Sigma k}\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge T_{2})

is homotopy right-invertible
(because
there is a canonical map
r

of the join
T_{1}*T_{2}

to
\Sigma(T_{1}\times T_{2})

such that

\Sigma k\circ r
is a homotopy equivalence).
Let
Y
be a space.
Given homotopy classes
\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\in[\Sigma T_{i},Y]
,

i=1,2
,
consider the homotopy classes

\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}:\Sigma(T_{1}\times T_{2})\xrightarrow{\Sigma p_{i}}\Sigma T_{i}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{a}_{i}}Y,\qquad i=1,2,

and
their commutator

\llbracket\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\circ\Sigma p_{1},\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\circ\Sigma p_{2}\rrbracket\in[\Sigma(T_{1}\times T_{2}),Y].

The Whitehead product

\lfloor\boldsymbol{a}_{1},\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\rceil\in[\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge T_{2}),Y]

is uniquely defined by (homotopy) commutativity of the diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\times T_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\llbracket\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\circ\Sigma p_{1},\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\circ\Sigma p_{2}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma k}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge T_{2}),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lfloor\boldsymbol{a}_{1},\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\rceil}

see
[
5
, Section 7.8]
.

Nested Whitehead products.

Let

T_{i}
,

i\in(s)
,
be compact cellular spaces
and

T_{i}\xleftarrow{p_{i}}T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s}\xrightarrow{k}T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s}

be the projections.

The map

\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s})\xrightarrow{\Sigma k}\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s})

is homotopy right-invertible.

Induction on
s
.
If
s=1
,

k
is the identity.
Take
s>1
.
Put

T^{\prime}=T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s-1},\qquad Z^{\prime}=T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s-1}.

Let

T^{\prime}\times T_{s}\xrightarrow{K}T^{\prime}\wedge T_{s}\qquad\text{and}\qquad T^{\prime}\xrightarrow{k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}

be the projections.
We have the decomposition

\Sigma k:\Sigma(T^{\prime}\times T_{s})\xrightarrow{\Sigma K}\Sigma(T^{\prime}\wedge T_{s})\xrightarrow{\Sigma(k^{\prime}\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T_{s}})}\Sigma(Z^{\prime}\wedge T_{s}),

where

\Sigma K
is right-invertible
(as noted in the beginning of § 25)
and
the second arrow is because
it coincides with

\Sigma T^{\prime}\wedge T_{s}\xrightarrow{\Sigma k^{\prime}\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T_{s}}}\Sigma Z^{\prime}\wedge T_{s},

which is right-invertible because

\Sigma k^{\prime}
is
by the induction hypothesis.
∎

Let

Y

be space
and

\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\in[\Sigma T_{i},Y]
,

i\in(s)
,
be homotopy classes.
Given a nesting
t
of weight
s
,
define the
t
-nested Whitehead product

\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}\in\lfloor\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s}),Y]

(by induction on
s
)
to be
either

\boldsymbol{a}_{1}

if
s=1
,
or

\lfloor\,\prescript{t^{\prime}\!}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{|t^{\prime}|},\,\prescript{t^{\prime\prime}\!}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=|t^{\prime}|+1}^{s}\,\rceil

if
t=(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
.

Consider the homotopy classes

\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}:\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s})\xrightarrow{\Sigma p_{i}}\Sigma T_{i}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{a}_{i}}Y,\qquad i\in(s).

For a nesting
t
of weght
s
,
the diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{c}:=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma k}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{w}:=\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}}

is (homotopy) commutative.

Induction on
s
.
If
s=1
,

\Sigma k
is the identity
and

\boldsymbol{c}=\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{a}_{1}
.
Take
s>1
.
We have
t=(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
.
Put

s^{\prime}=|t^{\prime}|
,

s^{\prime\prime}=|t^{\prime\prime}|
,
and

\displaystyle T^{\prime}

\displaystyle=T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s^{\prime}},

\displaystyle T^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=T_{s^{\prime}+1}\times\dotso\times T_{s},

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s^{\prime}},

\displaystyle Z^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=T_{s^{\prime}+1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s}.

We have the commutative diagrams of projections

\textstyle{T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{p_{i}}
\textstyle{T_{i}}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\prime}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{k^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime},}

\textstyle{T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{p_{i}}
\textstyle{T_{i}}
\textstyle{T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{k^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime\prime}.}

Consider the diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma(T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma P^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{c}}^{\prime}:=\prescript{t^{\prime}\!}{}{\llbracket}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\Sigma T^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prescript{t^{\prime}\!}{}{\llbracket}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p^{\prime}_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma k^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\Sigma Z^{\prime}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}:=\prescript{t^{\prime}\!}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s^{\prime}}}

(28)

The upper triangle is commutative because
the function

[\Sigma T^{\prime},Y]\to[\Sigma(T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime\prime}),Y]

induced by
\Sigma P^{\prime}

is a homomorphism
and sends
\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p^{\prime}_{i}
to
\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}
.
The lower triangle is commutative
by the induction hypothesis.
Similarly,
we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma(T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma P^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{c}}^{\prime\prime}:=\prescript{t^{\prime\prime}\!}{}{\llbracket}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}\rrbracket_{i=s^{\prime}+1}^{s}}
\textstyle{\Sigma T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prescript{t^{\prime\prime}\!}{}{\llbracket}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p^{\prime\prime}_{i}\rrbracket_{i=s^{\prime}+1}^{s}}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma k^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\Sigma Z^{\prime\prime}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime\prime}:=\prescript{t^{\prime\prime}\!}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=s^{\prime}+1}^{s}}

(29)

We have the commutative diagram of projections

\textstyle{T^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k^{\prime}}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{P^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{k^{\prime}\times k^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{k}
\textstyle{T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime}\times Z^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{Q^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{K}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime}\wedge Z^{\prime\prime}.}

Consider the diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma(T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma(k^{\prime}\times k^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{c}=\llbracket\widetilde{\boldsymbol{c}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{c}}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma k}
\textstyle{\Sigma(Z^{\prime}\times Z^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\llbracket\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\circ\Sigma Q^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime\prime}\circ\Sigma Q^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma K}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\Sigma(Z^{\prime}\wedge Z^{\prime\prime}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{w}=\lfloor\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime\prime}\rceil}

The upper triangle is commutative because
the function

[\Sigma(Z^{\prime}\times Z^{\prime\prime}),Y]\to[\Sigma(T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime\prime}),Y]

induced by
\Sigma(k^{\prime}\times k^{\prime\prime})

is a homomorphism
under which

\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\circ\Sigma Q^{\prime}\mapsto\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\circ\Sigma k^{\prime}\circ\Sigma P^{\prime}=\hfill\text{(by diagram \eqref{w'})}\hfill=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{c}}^{\prime}

and

\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime\prime}\circ\Sigma Q^{\prime\prime}\mapsto\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime\prime}\circ\Sigma k^{\prime\prime}\circ\Sigma P^{\prime\prime}=\hfill\text{(by diagram \eqref{w''})}\hfill=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{c}}^{\prime\prime}.

The lower triangle is commutative
by the definition of Whitehead product.
We are done.
∎

Let
R
be a homotopy right-inverse of
\Sigma k
:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 34.75177pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.75177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.68752pt\raise 5.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Sigma k}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.75177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 58.75177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 88.75177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.86063pt\raise-22.61383pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 16.74194pt\raise-8.05728pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\qquad\Sigma k\circ R\sim{\mathrm{id}}.

Then,
for any nesting
t
of weght
s
,
the diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{c}:=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{w}:=\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}}

is (homotopy) commutative.

We have

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\circ R=\hfill\text{(by Lemma\penalty\ 25.2)}\hfill=\boldsymbol{w}\circ\Sigma k\circ R=\hfill\text{(since $\Sigma k\circ R\sim{\mathrm{id}}$)}\hfill=\boldsymbol{w}.\displaymath@qed

§ 26. Loops and Whitehead products

Consider the wedge

B_{s}=\bigvee_{i\in(s)}S^{1}.

Given
a loop
v:S^{1}\to B_{s}

and
a space
T
,
introduce the map
v^{\Sigma}
:

\textstyle{\Sigma T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{\bigvee\limits_{i\in(s)}\Sigma T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{1}\wedge T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v\wedge{\mathrm{id}}_{T}}
\textstyle{B_{s}\wedge T.}

Let

{\mathrm{in}}_{j}:S^{1}\to B_{s}\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathrm{in}}^{T}_{j}:\Sigma T\to\bigvee_{i\in(s)}\Sigma T,\qquad j\in(s),

be the canonical insertions.

The function

\pi_{1}(B_{s})\to[\Sigma T,\bigvee_{i\in(s)}\Sigma T],\qquad[v]\mapsto[v^{\Sigma}],

is a homomorhism,
under which
[{\mathrm{in}}_{i}]\mapsto[{\mathrm{in}}^{T}_{i}]
.
∎

Let
T_{i}
,

i\in(s)
,
be spaces
and

T_{i}\xleftarrow{p_{i}}T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s}\xrightarrow{k}T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s}

be the projections.
Let

Y

be a space
and

a_{i}:\Sigma T_{i}\to Y

be maps.
We have the compositions

a_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}:\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dots\times T_{s})\xrightarrow{\Sigma p_{i}}\Sigma T_{i}\xrightarrow{a_{i}}Y.

Let
t
be a nesting of weight
s
.
Let
e:S^{1}\to B_{s}
be a loop with

[e]=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[{\mathrm{in}}_{i}]\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}

in
\pi_{1}(B_{s})
.
Then
the diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dots\times T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e^{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{c}:=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[a_{i}]\circ\Sigma p_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}}
\textstyle{\bigvee\limits_{i\in(s)}\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dots\times T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A:=\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{i\in(s)}a_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}}
\textstyle{Y.}

is (homotopy) commutative.

Put
T=T_{1}\times\dotso T_{s}
.
By Lemma 26.1,
the function

\pi_{1}(B_{s})\to[T,\bigvee_{i\in(s)}\Sigma T],\qquad[v]\mapsto[v^{\Sigma}],

is a homomorhism,
under which
[{\mathrm{in}}_{i}]\mapsto[{\mathrm{in}}^{T}_{i}]
.
Thus

[e^{\Sigma}]=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[{\mathrm{in}}^{T}_{i}]\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}.

The map
A
induces a homomorphism

[\Sigma T,\bigvee_{i\in(s)}\Sigma T]\to[\Sigma T,Y],

under which
[{\mathrm{in}}^{T}_{i}]\mapsto[a_{i}]\circ\Sigma p_{i}

and
thus

[e^{\Sigma}]=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[{\mathrm{in}}^{T}_{i}]\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}\mapsto\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[a_{i}]\circ\Sigma p_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}=\boldsymbol{c},

which is what was to be shown.
∎

By Lemma 25.1,
we have the diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dotso\times T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma k}
\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{R}

where
\Sigma k\circ R\sim{\mathrm{id}}
.
For a loop
v:S^{1}\to B_{s}
,
introduce the composition
\prescript{v}{R}{\lfloor}a_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}
:

\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dots\times T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{\bigvee_{i\in(s)}\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dots\times T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A:=\mathop{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hrule height=0.60275pt\vskip 0.69318pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.69318pt\hrule height=0.60275pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.45206pt\vskip 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.75346pt\hrule height=0.45206pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.39182pt\vskip 0.63293pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.63293pt\hrule height=0.39182pt}}{\vbox{\hrule height=0.27122pt\vskip 0.45206pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigvee$}\vskip 0.45206pt\hrule height=0.27122pt}}}}\limits_{i\in(s)}a_{i}\circ\Sigma p_{i}}
\textstyle{\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge T_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\prescript{v}{R}{\lfloor}a_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}}
\textstyle{Y.}

Let
t
be a nesting of weight
s
.
Let
e:S^{1}\to B_{s}
be a loop with

[e]=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[{\mathrm{in}}_{i}]\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}

in
\pi_{1}(B_{s})
.
Then

[\,\prescript{e}{R}{\lfloor}a_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}\,]=\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}[a_{i}]\rceil_{i=1}^{s}

in
[\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso\wedge T_{s}),Y]
.

Recall the homotopy class

\Sigma(T_{1}\times\dots\times T_{s})\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{c}:=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[a_{i}]\circ\Sigma p_{i}\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}}Y.

We have

[\prescript{e}{R}{\lfloor}a_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}]=[A\circ e^{\Sigma}\circ R]=\hfill\text{(by Lemma\penalty\ 26.2)}\qquad\\
\qquad=\boldsymbol{c}\circ R=\hfill\text{(by Corollary\penalty\ 25.3)}\hfill=\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}[a_{i}]\rceil_{i=1}^{s}.\displaymath@qed

§ 27. Strong nullarity of Whitehead products

Let
a_{i}:\Sigma T_{i}\to Y
,
etc.,
be as in § 26.

Let
v:S^{1}\to B_{s}
be a loop such that

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}v
.
Then

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}\prescript{v}{R}{\lfloor}a_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}.

We have

\prescript{v}{R}{\lfloor}a_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}=A\circ v^{\Sigma}\circ R

(see the construction).
By Corollary 5.4,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}v^{\Sigma}
.
By Corollary 5.2,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{r}{\approx}A\circ v^{\Sigma}\circ R
.
∎

Given
a nesting
t
of weight
s

and
homotopy classes
\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\in[\Sigma T_{i},Y]
,

i\in(s)
,
consider the
t
-nested Whitehead product

\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}\in[\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso T_{s}),Y].

One has

\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}\in[\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso T_{s}),Y]^{((s))}.

For each
i
,
choose a representative
a_{i}:\Sigma T_{i}\to Y
of
\boldsymbol{a}_{i}
.
Choose a loop
e:S^{1}\to B_{s}
with

[e]=\prescript{t}{}{\llbracket}[{\mathrm{in}}_{i}]\rrbracket_{i=1}^{s}

in
\pi_{1}(B_{s})
.
Clearly,
the loop
e
is Brunnian.
By Lemma 23.1,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{s-1}{\approx}e
.
By Lemma 27.1,

{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\displaystyle\raisebox{4.2625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{<}$}\mkern-4.1mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.53706pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}\mkern-4.4mu{|}}}\overset{s-1}{\approx}\prescript{e}{R}{\lfloor}a_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}.

By Lemma 26.3,

[\,\prescript{e}{R}{\lfloor}a_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}\,]=\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}.

Thus

\prescript{t}{}{\lfloor}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}\rceil_{i=1}^{s}\in[\Sigma(T_{1}\wedge\dotso T_{s}),Y]^{((s))}.\displaymath@qed
